--- Commit Message ---
LU-19921 target: add gssiam_server tunables in tgt_lproc.c

This patch adds the new sptlrpc.gssiam_server kobject and target
GSSIAM tunables (gssiam_auth_upcall, gssiam_auth_downcall,
gssiam_refresh_interval, gssiam_flush), along with the helper
tgt_gssiam_downcall() in tgt_gssiam.c and its sysfs wrapper in
tgt_lproc.c.

It also moves existing target-level tunables (LUSTRE_RW_ATTR and
LUSTRE_WO_ATTR) from tgt_main.c into tgt_lproc.c to keep
lprocfs/sysfs code in its own file, converting sprintf() calls to
scnprintf() along the way.

Seven new manual pages are added (gssiam_auth_upcall.4,
gssiam_auth_downcall.4, gssiam_refresh_interval.4, gssiam_flush.4,
sync_lock_cancel.4, tgt_fmd_count.4, tgt_fmd_seconds.4), and
module-specific redirects/symlinks are updated accordingly.

Signed-off-by: Di Wang <ddiwang@google.com>
Change-Id: Icf5025126c612a67e8fee3e375fb5aaade9a4231

(7 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some concerns, see inline comments.

The pure code move (tgt_main.c -> tgt_lproc.c plus the Makefile line) is still bundled with the brand-new GSSIAM tunables and man pages. Splitting the move out under its own Change-Id, marked trivial, was suggested on patchset 7 and would let it land on master independently of the rest of the series.

The four new sysfs tunables under sptlrpc.gssiam_server have no test coverage anywhere in the series - should sanity-sec grow a case that writes and reads them back?

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-08-11 15:59:47.000000000) ---
Patch Set 65:

(4 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Mostly good, comments inline; the code move itself is faithful (verified hunk-by-hunk against tgt_main.c, only sprintf() -> scnprintf() differs), and the new sptlrpc.gssiam_server group is wired up correctly (sptlrpc_init() runs before tgt_mod_init(), and tgt_gssiam_fini() removes the sysfs group before upcall_cache_cleanup()).

On patchset 7 Andreas asked for the tgt_main.c / tgt_lproc.c / Makefile code move to go out as its own Change-Id directly on master so it could land quickly as a trivial change. That still hasn't happened - the move is bundled here with the new sptlrpc.gssiam_server kobject, tgt_gssiam_downcall(), the tgt_gssiam_local_upcall() rework and seven man pages, and it sits at the tip of an unlanded series. Splitting it still looks worthwhile.

Two things outside the diff worth knowing: Documentation/Makefile.am's ALWAYS_SUBDIRS and config/lustre-build.m4's AC_CONFIG_FILES both omit man4 (and Documentation/man4 has no Makefile.am), so none of the seven new pages get installed - pre-existing, but it means this patch's documentation is currently unreachable via man. And nothing under lustre/tests references gssiam at all, so the four new tunables have no coverage; a sanity-sec case that sets and reads them back would be cheap.

Patch Set 83:

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Mostly good; the new code itself looks correct. Two whole-patch points: (1) the tgt_main.c -> tgt_lproc.c code motion plus the Makefile change is a self-contained, no-behavior-change refactor that has nothing to do with GSSIAM. It was suggested on an early patchset that this go in its own Change-Id on master so it can land quickly as a trivial patch, and it is still bundled here. The same applies to sync_lock_cancel.4, tgt_fmd_count.4 and tgt_fmd_seconds.4, which document tunables that have existed since 2.0.0 and 2.13.0 and are independent of this feature. Splitting those out would leave a much smaller GSSIAM change to review. (2) The four new sptlrpc.gssiam_server tunables are user-visible and nothing in the LU-19921 series exercises them. Could a sanity-sec case be added that at least reads gssiam_auth_upcall/gssiam_refresh_interval, checks that a bad gssiam_auth_downcall string is rejected, and that gssiam_flush=-1 succeeds while other values fail?
